## [Developer] 2026-08-12 — FIX-AUDITOR-A30-DEEPPROBE-TRUNCATES-ON-SECOND-CONTAINER-RAG-SERVICE (cross-service/, developer, high, PO triage dispatch)

3 consecutive Tier-1 cycles (c40/c41/c42, `docs/agent-memory/notebooks/system-auditor.md`) logged an honest DEFER for rag-service's A-30 deep-probe — "Probe output incomplete... raw JSON verdict block not present" — while the FIRST ENGAGEd container (pdf-extractor) always completed via the identical mechanism. AC-1 mandated REFUTATION-FIRST before any fix: a live reversed-order test (rag-service run FIRST, completed @81s elapsed; pdf-extractor run SECOND, would still be executing at the caller's own ~120s wall-time budget, finishing @~158s cumulative) reproduced the SAME position-dependent truncation seen in notebook c41 (pdf-extractor 1st completed / rag-service 2nd truncated) with the containers SWAPPED — REFUTES the container-specific premise in the originating report. **Root cause:** `_a30_run_investigate_gate()` (`docs/agents/system-auditor/probe.sh`) invoked every ENGAGEd container's deep-probe SEQUENTIALLY and SYNCHRONOUSLY inside one process; each deep-probe's own sample loop + docker inspect/exec overhead measured live at 77-81s for the 6-probe/13s-interval shape, so 2 ENGAGEd containers summed to ~150-160s — past `tier1-probe.md`'s own <120s wall-time target — and whichever container was probed SECOND was always the one still mid-sample-loop when the caller's external timeout elapsed, killed with ZERO buffered output. **Fix:** ENGAGEd containers' deep-probes now launch CONCURRENTLY (background jobs, `exec`'d into the real subprocess so a watchdog kill reaches the actual docker-sampling process, not an orphan-leaving wrapper shell — verified empirically) with a per-container watchdog deadline (`(PROBES-1)*INTERVAL + 30s margin`, all named/overridable vars, not re-hardcoded). A container that blows its own deadline now emits a structured `{"verdict":"INCONCLUSIVE","truncated":true}` JSON marker (AC-3) instead of an unstructured prose DEFER — print order stays deterministic (engage order) even though execution is parallel. `verify-a30-mcp-memory-reclamation.sh` itself is untouched (0 diff) — PROBES/INTERVAL are now named vars passed in from `probe.sh` instead of a hardcoded `6 13` literal. `tier1-probe.md` gets a new clause 2b (parses the INCONCLUSIVE/truncated marker as a structured DEFER) plus an explicit sequencing note for the CONFLICT board row `FIX-AUDITOR-A30-SUSTAINED-WINDOW-SHORTER-THAN-TARGET-RECLAMATION-PERIOD` (READY, same files, wants to LENGTHEN one container's own window) — this fix removes the sequential-summing hazard that made two containers' windows additive, but does not itself widen any single container's window; that row's own external-timeout recalibration is still its own scope. **Tests:** `probe.test.sh` 16→24 checks (8 new: T14 parallel-dispatch timing, T14b both-containers-present, T15/T15b/T15c/T15d watchdog-kill + AC-3 marker, T16 deterministic print order, T17 deadline arithmetic) — RED confirmed against pre-fix `probe.sh` (6/8 new checks failed: 2x3s stub took 6s not <5s, hung stub never got killed/no marker, deadline function missing), GREEN 24/24 post-fix. `verify-a30-mcp-memory-reclamation.test.sh` 15/15 unchanged (file untouched). **LIVE end-to-end proof** (real docker, both pdf-extractor 86.42% and rag-service 93.77% simultaneously ENGAGEd — no synthetic fixtures): 2 CONSECUTIVE full `bash docs/agents/system-auditor/probe.sh` cycles completed in 100s and 99s wall respectively (previously would have summed to ~150-160s and truncated), BOTH containers' full JSON verdict blocks present in EVERY cycle (pdf-extractor FOLD, rag-service ESCALATE — a separate, already-ACK'd finding via `FU-RAG-DEPLOY-MEMORY`, not this task's concern), zero DEFER (AC-2), sample timestamps for the two containers visibly overlapping in both runs, proving genuine parallel execution rather than merely parallel-looking code. `shellcheck` clean (no new warning class vs the pre-existing baseline). No `apps/` TS/Go touched (pure bash+md, zone `cross-service/`) — `bun test`/`tsc` structurally N/A. Commit `a9e03849a`. QA next.

## [Developer] 2026-08-07 — FIX-EMITSIGNAL-E3-RC1-OPAQUE-FATAL-DROPS-DETECTOR-FINDING (cross-service/, developer, P1, PO triage dispatch)

PO RAW-verified telegram BUG 4477/4478 (`[emit-signal] BUG: E-3 write/read-back failure rc=1`, check_id=A-21) as a TRANSIENT peer-write race — neither dropped signal id (`sys-20260807T014959-6afe`/`sys-20260807T015106-7023`) ever landed in `.signal_queue.rows[]`, the SAME actuator succeeded 72s later with zero code change, and `orch-validate.mjs` was green on every committed version spanning the window (only the uncommitted mid-write state failed). Root cause: `scripts/orch-apply.sh` exits 1 from THREE unrelated branches (validator abort :136-141, updated_at-stamp abort :159-164, conservation-abort :179-184) that `_e3_write_row()`'s final `else` collapsed into one opaque fatal-no-retry lane, and `_orch_apply_invoke() { bash "$ORCH_APPLY_SH"; }` discarded orch-apply's own diagnostic stderr — the BUG telegram carried only `rc=1`, zero reason. **Fix:** `_orch_apply_invoke()` now captures stderr into `ORCH_APPLY_STDERR`; `_e3_write_row()` greps it for the `"[orch-apply] ABORTED: ..."`/`"ERROR: ..."` reason line and (AC-1) threads it into both the `e3-write-failed`/`e3-cas-exhausted` marker and the anti-false-green BUG telegram, and (AC-2) routes a VALIDATOR-abort rc=1 into the SAME CAS-retry lane as rc=2 (its candidate is a live-file read that can race a peer mid-write) while a CONSERVATION/updated_at-stamp-abort rc=1 stays fatal, zero retry (its candidate is already live+exactly-one-appended-row — never a race). `rc` is deliberately left at its true value on a validator-race retry (never overwritten to 2) so an exhausted-after-3-attempts marker still reports the real underlying rc. **Tests:** new T32 (validator-abort transient race — retries, recovers on attempt 2, no ABORT marker, row lands) and T33 (conservation-abort — stays fatal, reason propagates into marker+telegram), direct siblings of the existing T25/T26 rc=3 fixtures. Both confirmed RED at HEAD (via `git stash` isolating the production diff), GREEN after. `emit-audit-signal.test.sh` 108/108 (98 pre-existing + 10 new assertions across T32/T33), zero-regression sweep on `db-integrity-history-append.test.sh` 28/28. `bash -n`/`shellcheck` clean. No `apps/` TS touched (zone `cross-service/`, pure bash) — `bun test`/`tsc` structurally N/A. QA next.

## [Developer] 2026-08-06 — FIX-EMITSIGNAL-E3-RC3-FATAL-NORETRY-DROPS-DETECTOR-FINDING (cross-service/, developer, P1, PO triage dispatch)

`scripts/emit-audit-signal.sh`'s `_e3_write_row()` CAS-retry loop retried rc=2 (mtime mismatch) but treated EVERYTHING else, including rc=3, as an immediate fatal abort — no retry. Per `scripts/orch-apply.sh`'s own exit-code contract (line 48), rc=3 = "usage error (empty stdin, live file missing, I/O error)", and one of those causes is transient: `candidate=$(jq ... "$ORCH_STATE_FILE")` yields an EMPTY string when that read races a concurrent peer rename of the live file, and empty stdin is exactly what `orch-apply.sh` exits 3 on — so a transient race got misclassified as a permanent usage error and the finding was silently dropped with zero retry (RAW evidence: 5 `scheduler_locks-FAIL` BUG telegrams fired today, 0 of the 5 ever landed a `signal_queue` row, 4/5 clustered in the tick's heaviest concurrent orch-state write window). **Fix (approach a, no `orch-apply.sh` contract change needed):** factored the jq read into a new `_e3_read_candidate()` seam; `_e3_write_row()` now checks candidate non-emptiness BEFORE ever invoking `_orch_apply_invoke` — an empty read is retried in the SAME lane as rc=2, never even reaching `orch-apply.sh`, so it can never generate a misleading rc=3. A GENUINE usage error (e.g. live file actually missing) can still only surface from a REAL `_orch_apply_invoke` call on a non-empty candidate, and that branch is untouched — still fatal, zero retry, per the fix directive's explicit "do not blanket-retry rc=3" constraint. **Tests:** new T25 (empty-candidate-then-recovers — RETRY, succeeds on attempt 2, no ABORT marker, row lands) and T26 (rc=3 from a non-empty candidate, e.g. the genuine missing-live-file usage error — NO retry, invoked exactly once, `ABORT e3-write-failed rc=3`, orphan BUG telegram) — both branches covered explicitly per `feedback_fleetwide_gate_validated_on_one_file_optout_allowlist`. TDD confirmed: T25 RED on the pre-fix code (T26 already GREEN pre-fix, locking in the sibling behavior that must NOT change). `emit-audit-signal.test.sh` 84/84 (82 pre-existing + 2 new), zero-regression sweep on `db-integrity-history-append.test.sh` 26/26, `db-integrity-dedup-check.test.sh` 13/13, `mcp-call.test.sh` 9/9. `scripts/orch-apply.sh` left untouched (read-only reference for the exit-code contract cited in the new comments) — no code change needed for approach (a). No `apps/` TS/Go touched (zone `cross-service/`, pure bash) — `bun test`/`tsc` structurally N/A. QA next.

## [Developer] 2026-08-06 — FIX-DB-INTEGRITY-SIDECAR-NAMED-VOLUME-DRIFT (PO QA-BLOCKING re-claim, AC-5/AC-6/AC-7)

PO declined sign-off on the first pass (commit `656a63821`): measured live, with `data/live/market.db-wal` at 1,545,032 bytes pending, that `immutable=1` silently returned a stale `daily_ohlcv` count (21 rows short, no error) AND the stale on-disk `journal_mode` (`'delete'`) while the DB was genuinely `'wal'` at that instant — the exact same blind-fail-open failure mode this row was minted to fix, in a different costume. New shared `scripts/lib/sqlite-wal-guard.sh`: `wal_guard_read_uri`/`wal_guard_read_mode` pick `file:<db>?immutable=1` iff `<db>-wal` is absent/0 bytes, else fall back to `file:<db>?mode=ro` — wired into all 3 observer scripts (`db-integrity-counts.sh`, `db-integrity-probe.sh`, `db-empty-table-classify.sh`), each now reports `read_mode` in its own JSON output. AC-6: deleted all 3 "always safe" source comments plus the same claim in the 3 live cron prompt docs (`cron-db-data-integrity.md` + both `register-job-db-integrity-*.md` mirrors), replaced with the conditional rule. AC-7: `journal_mode` is never read through `immutable=1` anywhere; a regression test asserts none of the 3 scripts query it in executable code — the only sanctioned path stays `scripts/audits/verify-market-db-journal-mode.sh`. New `scripts/lib/sqlite-wal-hold-open-fixture.sh` (bash-3.2-safe FIFO-fed held-open reader, no `coproc`) lets every new test DELIBERATELY reproduce a non-empty `-wal` instead of waiting for one, per the board row's verification gate. Verified 4 layers deep (`sqlite-wal-guard.test.sh` 15/15, `db-integrity-counts.test.sh` 23/23, `db-integrity-probe.test.sh` 33/33 — incl. a REAL subprocess invocation proving the live script emits SPAWN on a real committed change a bare `immutable=1` open would have silently missed as SKIP-SPAWN — `db-empty-table-classify.test.sh` 40/40) plus a genuinely-live check against `data/live/market.db` (`-wal` non-empty, real production writers, not synthetic): all 3 scripts self-report `read_mode:"ro"` unprompted, and a direct `immutable=1` vs `mode=ro` `PRAGMA journal_mode` cross-check at the same instant reproduced the exact PO defect (`'delete'` vs `'wal'`, corroborated by `verify-market-db-journal-mode.sh`'s own container-path reader). Zero regressions across the 4 pre-existing sibling suites (mount-drift-check 12/12, history-append 26/26, dedup-check 13/13, emit-audit-signal 75/75) — 237/237 total across all 8 suites. No `apps/` TS/Go touched (zone `cross-service/`, pure bash+md) — `bun test`/`tsc` structurally N/A. QA next.

system-auditor's C-08 (orphaned alerts) checked a 24h alerts window against an `agent_signals`
correlation stub with a fixed 2h TTL (purged only once daily) — `expected=0` was mathematically
unsatisfiable, and an ISO8601 strcmp bypass (`alerts.triggered_at` T-format vs `datetime('now',...)`
space-format) independently over-captured rows. Rebound the window to 2h (the exact co-write TTL
literal, `alertStore.ts:223`) and wrapped both sides in `datetime()`, copying `checkStaleAlerts.ts`'s
already-proven pattern. Fleet-audited every C-xx timestamp-vs-`datetime('now',...)` check in
`docs/agents/system-auditor/flow/main.md`; also fixed C-04/C-07/C-10/C-11 (same T-format class),
left C-06/C-09/C-16 unwrapped (measured 0% T-format, no mixed-writer exposure). Retracted two
fabricated root-cause entries in `docs/data/DASHBOARD.md` (unmeasured "pipeline delayed"/"not
propagated" narration, now DISCONFIRMED — the alert-engine write path is healthy). Kept C-08 active
rather than retiring it in favor of `checkOrphanAgentSignalsAlertId` (D-NEW2), which only covers the
inverse FK direction. New doc-driven regression test (reads the live SQL out of the flow doc, not a
hardcoded copy) — `FIX-AUDITOR-C08-UNSATISFIABLE-TTL-WINDOW-AND-ISO8601-STRCMP.test.ts`, 5/5 pass.

## [Developer] 2026-07-31 — FIX-NOTEBOOK-AUTOPRUNE-DIRECTION-UNRESOLVABLE-ZERO-TS-NOTEBOOKS

`scripts/agents-flow/notebook-auto-prune.sh`'s same-day/tied-heading direction vote (dec/inc on
adjacent `## ` section ts_keys) structurally deadlocked (TIE_COUNT>=2, dec=0, inc=0, forever) for any
file where EVERY retained section lacks a parseable timestamp — non-chronological/rolling sections
(e.g. `## Known patterns / preferences`, `## Identity`), not per-cycle dated journal entries. The
`docs/data/notebook-section-order.json` override table (introduced by the sibling fix
FIX-NOTEBOOK-AUTOPRUNE-SAMEDAY-TIE-DROPS-NEWEST) covered only 3 files and had no producer, so all 11
confirmed-live deadlocked files (code-janitor, cowork-refactory-expert x2, digest-predict, idea-forge,
market-analyst, ops-mainserver-fetch, pm-alpha-s2-rag-fts-rebuild-cron, po, semble-search,
dev-technical-analysis) fail-louded at the hook's tie-break and exited 0 WITHOUT pruning — caps grew
unchecked (11 fail-loud signals in ~35h across 2 files: unified-agent.md, digest-predict.md). PO's
explicit preference (row): prefer an opt-IN derivation that always resolves over growing the
opt-out override table one file at a time (`feedback_fleetwide_gate_validated_on_one_file_optout_allowlist`).
Fix: (1) TERTIARY hardening — the vote no longer casts a phantom vote when either side of a pair is
the untimestamped MAX-sentinel (a trailing rolling heading, e.g. `## Prior cycles`, was silently
injecting a spurious "increasing" vote against the real timestamp before it); (2) PRIMARY — when
neither the file's own votes NOR the override table give any signal (this is now provably permanent
for a given file shape, not transient), the direction case-statement's default arm applies a
documented, deterministic default (`newest_first`: drop the physically-LAST tied section) instead of
refusing to prune forever, and emits a non-blocking INFORMATIONAL signal
(`notebook_tiebreak_direction_defaulted`, glob `docs/signals/notebook-direction-defaulted-*.json`) —
NOT a breach — recording that the default was used; (3) AC-3 — the `[ "$prev_key" \> "$cur_key" ]`
vote comparison (a bash-`[`-builtin extension; under zsh's `[` it hard-errors and every differing
pair silently inverts to the opposite direction, dropping the true-newest tied section — silent data
loss) is replaced with a POSIX numeric `-gt` test, behaviorally identical for the fixed-width all-digit
ts_key strings involved but portable across bash/zsh/dash — new Test 9 in
`scripts/agents-flow/test-notebook-auto-prune.sh` proves the outcome is now IDENTICAL whether the
script is invoked under bash or zsh (RED pre-fix, GREEN post-fix). `docs/data/notebook-section-order.json`
kept (still a useful manually-verified precision override for the 2 known append-style files it
correctly overrides) but made genuinely non-load-bearing (AC-2: the derivation never NEEDS it now) —
`_maintained_by`/`_note` corrected to state the true contract (manual-only, as-needed, intentionally
no automated producer — a wrong automated entry would silently invert the drop direction, worse than
no entry). New reusable verification tool: `scripts/agents-flow/notebook-direction-corpus-replay.sh`
(AC-1 — replays the derivation over the WHOLE 46-file live corpus in an isolated sandbox, forcing a
full drain to exercise every tie depth; 21/46 deadlocked pre-fix (superset of the 11 named, since the
forced full-drain reaches sub-ties the files' CURRENT content doesn't yet expose) → 0/46 post-fix, with
an isolated vote-logic diff confirming zero regression in the FINAL direction for all 25 already-
correctly-resolving files). AC-4 live convergence: ran the fixed hook for real against
`docs/agent-memory/notebooks/unified-agent.md` (197L/25703B → 90L/11562B) and `digest-predict.md`
(192L/51298B → 33L/9508B) — both now under the live 200L/12000B cap (`docs/data/file-size-caps.json`);
retained sections verified byte-identical to their pre-prune slices (only whole sections dropped, per
the existing immutability contract). Tests: `notebook-auto-prune.test.sh` 7/7 (T7 updated — its old
assertion encoded the exact "refuse to guess forever" anti-pattern this task closes), sibling
`test-notebook-auto-prune.sh` 9/9 (new Test 9). Decision journal:
`docs/agent-memory/decisions/sprint-COWORK-GUARANTEED-SLOT-CATCHUP-developer.md`.

## [Developer] 2026-07-31 — FIX-COWORK-FIRE-ELECTION-TICK-TOMBSTONE

Fixed the production double-fire bug (confirmed twice in 27h, 2026-07-30T21:00Z + 2026-07-31T00:00Z):
`cron:cowork:<TICK>` was released at end of Step 6, so a re-fire of the same completed tick found the
key free and re-elected/re-ran from scratch. Added a pre-election tombstone check (new `_tick_already_ran()`
predicate + new Step 2.5 in `scripts/agents-flow/cowork-tick-preflight.sh`) comparing `pressure-state.json`'s
`tick_id` (server second-precision) against the nominal tick (script minute-precision), normalized by
stripping the trailing `:SS` (NFR-1 landmine — a naive `==` is always false). New `TOMBSTONED` verdict makes
ZERO `task_claim` calls on a tombstoned tick (AC-1, tool-call level). Mirrored the same check in
`docs/agents/cowork-team/flow/leader-lock.md` (ERROR-fallback path, no shared runtime to reuse the bash
predicate). Added `TOMBSTONED` + unrecognized-verdict fail-safe rows to `main.md`'s JUMP-TO table, an
NFR-3 ordering-invariant comment pin in `telemetry.md`, and a `TOMBSTONED` clause + rollout note in
`.claude/skills/cron-cowork-team/SKILL.md` (bare `/cron-cowork-team` re-run is a no-op post-fix — needs
explicit `CronDelete`+`CronCreate`). `cowork-tick-preflight.test.sh` extended with 2 positive-control
tests replaying the two real incident timestamps verbatim (must assert `true`, not just "suite exits 0"),
negative controls, an end-to-end TOMBSTONED case, and an NFR-2 no-over-suppression regression — 40/40 pass.

## [Developer] 2026-07-30 — FIX-DECISION-JOURNAL-SKILL-CAPCHECK-LINE-ONLY-NO-BYTE-ROLLOVER

`.claude/skills/decision-journal/SKILL.md` § Cap Check tested only `LINES>600`, so a journal under
600 lines but well over the 36000-byte cap (dense long lines) never rolled — root cause of a
recurring `context_bloat_breach` signal (2 fired 16 min apart the triage tick that minted this
row), all on journals well under 600L. Third instance of this defect class in the repo, same fix
shape as the already-QA-approved sibling `FIX-NOTEBOOK-PRUNER-LINE-ONLY-SETPOINT-BYTE-CAP-NEVER-CONVERGES`
(`scripts/agents-flow/notebook-auto-prune.sh`): Cap Check now trips on `LINES>LINE_CAP OR
BYTES>BYTE_CAP`; `LINE_CAP` is read from `docs/data/file-size-caps.json` (SSOT, pattern
`docs/agent-memory/decisions/sprint-*.md`, cap=600), `BYTE_CAP=LINE_CAP*60` (=36000) — the SAME
derivation `context-bloat-backstop.sh` uses (TE-T24), never a second hardcoded `36000`. Rollover
target is now a numeric-suffix increment parsed off the current `$JOURNAL_PATH` (base file =
implicit index 1) instead of a hardcoded `-2.md`, so a breaching `-2.md` now rolls to `-3.md`,
unbounded, closing AC(5) (no `-3.md` path existed before this fix). Verified by extracting the
patched bash verbatim and running it against synthetic fixtures using the real
`docs/data/file-size-caps.json`: 300L/79800B → rolls (byte axis); 650L/19500B → rolls (line axis
not regressed); 300L/19800B → untouched (both caps clear); synthetic `-2.md` at 620L → rolls to
`-3.md`; synthetic `-3.md` at 620L → rolls to `-4.md` (confirms unbounded, not a hardcoded pair).
Applied live to this cycle's own `docs/agent-memory/decisions/sprint-COWORK-GUARANTEED-SLOT-CATCHUP-developer.md`
journal (499L/159,241B — exactly the blind-spot shape cited in the task, one of the 3 named
examples) — appended the `CAP-REACHED` sentinel per the fixed logic; the mandatory `send_telegram`
bug-channel notification could NOT be sent this session (no MCP/gateway tool grant — Read/Edit/
Write/Bash only), flagged for a human/PO to send or for the next gateway-capable cycle to catch up
on. Not implemented (explicitly out of this row's scope, owned by a separate blocked P1 backlog
row `FIX-DECISION-JOURNAL-BYTECAP-NO-ACTUATOR`): an automated sweep/actuator over the other ~10
fleet-wide journals already sitting in this blind spot — this fix only corrects the prose/logic an
agent follows on its OWN next journal write. No `apps/` TS/Go touched (zone `cross-service/`, pure
`.claude/skills/` md+bash) — `bun test`/`tsc` N/A. File itself: 99L/4349B, well under its own
governed `.claude/skills/**/*.md` cap (200L/12000B).

## [Developer] 2026-07-30 — FIX-NOTEBOOK-DUPHEADING-DETECTOR-NO-DEDUP-NO-ACTUATOR

`docs/agent-memory/notebooks/unified-agent.md` had two adjacent `## Prior cycles` headings with only a blank line between them (zero content by construction — PO adjudication: provably lossless to collapse), orphaning the `**Last updated:** 2026-07-29T05:23:00Z ...` footer under the second, and `scripts/agents-flow/notebook-auto-prune.sh`'s `emit_dup_signal()` had no dedup (no state file at all) and no actuator (`action_required: manual_review`, never consumed) — 6 near-identical signals fired for the same file+heading on 2026-07-30 alone, burning drain-cycle slots. Fix, scoped exactly to the PO's adjudicated exception (every OTHER duplicate-heading shape — real content between the pair, non-adjacent duplicates — stays detection-only/unchanged): (1) `repair_adjacent_dup_heading()`/`detect_dup_heading_lines()` now auto-collapse ONLY the shape `detect_dup_heading()` already matched (adjacent identical `## ` heading, nothing but blanks between) — deletes the first occurrence, wired into both the pre-cap-scan and post-prune-scan call sites; (2) new persistent marker ledger (`docs/data/notebook-dup-heading-signal-state/`, keyed by file+heading) via `emit_dup_signal_deduped()`/`_dup_clear_markers_for_file()` — emits at most once while the corrupted condition persists, clears the moment a scan finds the file clean, re-arming for a later genuinely-new recurrence. Live file manually repaired to match (`grep -c '^## Prior cycles'` == 1, footer reattached, lossless). Tests: `scripts/agents-flow/test-notebook-auto-prune.sh` Test 5 updated (was detection-only/byte-identical assertion — now asserts auto-repair, that's the behavior being overturned) + new Tests 6/7 (content-between and non-adjacent duplicates stay byte-identical/unfixed) + new Test 8 (unit-level dedup-ledger contract: 1 signal on fresh corruption, 0 on a same-still-uncleared re-check, 1 again after clear+recur) — 8/8 GREEN. No regressions: `scripts/agents-flow/notebook-auto-prune.test.sh` (byte/line-cap + sameday-tie suite) 7/7 still GREEN. OUT OF SCOPE (flagged for `agent-father` follow-up, not implemented here): an anchor-uniqueness invariant for permanent-accumulator headings in `.claude/skills/notebook-write/SKILL.md` (`.md` agent-contract edit — belongs to the agent-md-factory, not a code developer) and the root cause itself (`docs/agents/unified-agent/flow/chef.md:837`'s AC-2b intra-prune re-emitting the `## Prior cycles` anchor without an existence check — different task/zone). Forward gate (cannot be proven inside this single invocation): zero NEW `docs/signals/notebook-duplicate-heading-*unified-agent*` files across at least 2 subsequent chef cycles — dev-team/QA should check.

## [Developer] 2026-07-30 — FIX-AUDITOR-OUTPUT-CONTRACT-SIGNALSPOSTED-COUNTS-CALLS-NOT-CONFIRMED-ROWS

Empirically established (2 in-process repro scripts, run before any code change) BOTH mechanisms behind the 07:09Z Tier-2 auditor's `signals_posted=3` while `agent_signals` had a hard zero-row gap: (a) `postSignalWithCriticGate`/`postSignal` return sentinel `signalId=-1` for EVERY dedup-suppressed no-op, not just the critic-first-reject case — `agentSignalTools.ts`'s `post_agent_signal` handler only special-cased that one narrow branch, so any OTHER `id<=0` (identical-payload `INSERT OR IGNORE` guard, or the Task-1862g same-direction time-window guard) fell through to `success:true` with the bare `-1` embedded as a fake row id; (b) a genuine thrown DB error (simulated live via a monkeypatched `db.prepare` forcing the real INSERT to throw) returns `Error: <message>` TEXT without `isError:true` — the only one of the handler's 3 non-success paths that omitted it — and `scripts/agents-flow/mcp-call.sh`'s `_mcp_call_parse()` inspected only `.result.isError`, treating it as success. Fix: (1) tool layer — any `id<=0` now returns `success:false`; catch-all sets `isError:true`; (2) `mcp-call.sh` — `_mcp_call_parse()` also treats an `Error:`-prefix on response text as failure even when `isError` is unset (shared fix, benefits every `mcp_call()` caller); (3) `scripts/emit-audit-signal.sh`'s `_run_e1()` no longer trusts `mcp_call` rc alone — parses the JSON body for `success===true` + positive `signal_id` (new `ABORT e1-not-written`), then performs a MANDATORY read-back via `get_agent_signals` confirming the id is actually present in `agent_signals` before counting toward `signals_posted` (new `ABORT e1-readback-failed`, non-dedup BUG telegram — mirrors the existing E-3 signal_queue read-back pattern, applied to the other store this script writes to). Both new ABORT reasons fall under `audit-output-contract.sh`'s existing wildcard — no parser change needed. Tests: new `FIX-AUDITOR-OUTPUT-CONTRACT-SIGNALSPOSTED-COUNTS-CALLS-NOT-CONFIRMED-ROWS.test.ts` (2/2), new `scripts/agents-flow/mcp-call.test.sh` (9/9), extended `scripts/emit-audit-signal.test.sh` (67/67, T16-T20 new). No regressions: agent-signal-family TS suite 114/114, `audit-output-contract.test.sh` 35/35, `mcp-call-gateway-meta.test.sh` 20/20, `tsc` clean. SIBLING (batch, not merged): `FIX-AUDITOR-DASHBOARD-APPEND-NO-ACTUATOR-CONTRACT-COUNT-NARRATED` — same narrated-count class, dashboard-append surface, already in review; QA reviews both together.

Live-diagnosed `/proxy/ssc-insider` HTTP 502 without needing VPS SSH: `congbothongtin.ssc.gov.vn` returns HTTP 503 "No server is available to handle this request" on EVERY path (including domain root), reproduced identically both directly (non-VN egress) and via the VPS proxy (VN egress) — rules out geo-block and VPS/proxy misconfig. Parent `ssc.gov.vn` front (nginx) is healthy, but the underlying WebCenter/WebLogic application tier itself (`ssc.gov.vn/webcenter/portal/ubck`) times out entirely — same app tier `congbothongtin` runs on. Added an in-code comment at `vps-scripts/vps-proxy-server.js`'s `SSC_INSIDER_UPSTREAM` documenting both findings so a future agent doesn't re-discover and re-revert the same retry. Updated `docs/architecture-briefs/2026-07-01-BA-PREDICTION-EVIDENCE-REVIVAL.md` with the closure. ## [Developer] 2026-07-29 — FIX-AUDITOR-HEARTBEAT-OUT-OF-CONTRACT-AGENT-WRITE-TIER1

## [Developer] 2026-07-28 — FIX-NOTEBOOK-PRUNER-LINE-ONLY-SETPOINT-BYTE-CAP-NEVER-CONVERGES

`scripts/agents-flow/notebook-auto-prune.sh` early-exit/loop-break were line-only (≤200L); the byte cap (LINE_CAP × 60 = 12000B, TE-T24) had a detector (`context-bloat-backstop.sh`) but no actuator, so densely-written notebooks (e.g. alert-commander.md, 632 bytes/line) never got pruned. Pruner now derives LINE_CAP/BYTE_CAP from the same SSOT (`docs/data/file-size-caps.json`) and stops only when BOTH lines ≤ cap AND bytes ≤ cap. ## [Developer] 2026-07-21 — FIX-ORCHSTATE-UPDATED-AT-WRITE-PATH: `scripts/orch-apply.sh` had zero timestamp handling — task_board row `updated_at` was stamped only by whichever ad-hoc jq caller happened to remember, leaving most rows permanently null. New Stage 1.5 (`scripts/orch-stamp-updated-at.mjs`, runs AFTER Stage 0/1 validation so the raw-text dup-key scan is unaffected) diffs every row by `id` against the live file, excluding `updated_at` itself from the comparison, and stamps `updated_at=now` ONLY on rows whose content actually changed (or are brand-new) — idempotent, no re-stamp on unchanged re-apply. Diff unit is deliberately lane-agnostic (a pure lane move with zero field change is not "changed"; real lane moves virtually always change `status`, which IS caught). No backfill of the ~500 pre-existing null rows — they age out naturally. ## [Developer] 2026-07-23 — UC-MDH-P4 (P1 FIX, memory-docs-hygiene-P4): implemented the sprint-journal archival that `docs/data/file-size-caps.json` had promised since inception ("Archived → docs/archive/decisions/ at sprint close by pm") but pm's task-archive flow never carried out. New `scripts/agents-flow/decision-journal-archive.sh` — file-ops-only (jq reads orch-state, never writes it) — moves `docs/agent-memory/decisions/sprint-<id>.md` / `sprint-<id>-<agent>.md` journals into `docs/archive/decisions/` once their sprint has CLOSED. Selection is STATUS-based (closed vs still-active sprint id), NOT mtime-based — this is deliberate (TE-T33's mtime rotation would wrongly move journals of sprints still open past 30d); the decisions/ leg of TE-T33 is now superseded by this script. Uses LONGEST-match id derivation, never bare-prefix glob: the repo has a live case where a closed id (`OHLCV-UNIT-CONTAM`) is a literal string-prefix of an active id (`OHLCV-UNIT-CONTAM-WHOLEROW-LT1000`) — bare-prefix matching would misfile the active sprint's journals. Two modes: stdin (per-cycle, caller pipes just-closed sprint ids) and `--all` (backfill, derives the full closed-id set from `docs/data/orch/archive/*.json` + hot `closed_sprints[]`). Wired into `docs/agents/pm/flow/task-archive.md` as new Step 5.5 (after Step 5 post-eviction verify, since sprints close via BOTH §Sprint Eviction and Step 4's `orch-cold-evict.sh`) with a pre-eviction id-capture line + Step 6's commit pathspec extended to cover both old+new journal paths; CANONICAL pointer added to `docs/policies/dev-standards.md` § Script Persistence. Paired `decision-journal-archive.test.sh` — 23/23 PASS, fully sandboxed (mktemp -d fixture tree, `DJA_GIT_MV=0`, never touches live `docs/agent-memory/decisions/` or `docs/data/orch/`) — covers the live prefix-collision shape, stdin-mode scoping, `--all` backfill, bare + agent-suffixed filename forms, no-orch-record files left in place and counted, mtime-independence, idempotent re-run, and destination-collision safety (SKIP-EXISTS, never overwrites). Did NOT run the one-time live backfill (mutating action, out of this FIX's scope) — verified read-only via `--dry-run` (new flag, previews `WOULD-ARCHIVE` with zero filesystem writes) against the live repo: scanned=431, archived(would)=202, active_stay=24, no_orch_record=205 (`git status`/file-count confirmed unchanged before/after); left as a follow-up PO-routed action. New `LAUNCHD_ACK_PATH` test seam (mirrors `LAUNCHD_DIR_PATH`); extended `auditor-tier1-probe.test.sh` with a default nonexistent-ledger seam (byte-identical T1-T35 regression, 102/102 still PASS) + 4 new cases T36-T39 (acked-only→ALL_GREEN, mixed acked+new→FAILURE, ledger-present-but-uncovered→FAILURE, all-healthy+ledger-present→ALL_GREEN no false noise) — 120/120 total PASS. The LIVE `get_system_status` MCP tool's "Auto-tracked Indicators" section runs its own raw, unguarded "latest row per indicator" SQL (no staleness check) and was confirmed serving `dow_jones  23750  (49 data points)` as if current — the exact value in report 3237. 3-layer fix: (1) new `infrastructure/db/indicatorPlausibility.ts` — generic, shared `isPlausibleIndicatorValue()` band gate (dow_jones 25000–60000) used by every `tracked_indicators` writer, not a one-off; `commodityTracker.ts` refactored to delegate to it (behavior-preserving for all other indicators — verified same bounds byte-for-byte, only dow_jones tightened). (3) `systemTools.ts get_system_status` "Auto-tracked Indicators" switched from its own unguarded query to the already-proven staleness-aware `listTrackedIndicatorsFromDb()` (DSI-MACRO-PHANTOM-STALE-GUARD/GUARD-7/8 precedent) — a stale row is now labelled `[STALE]`, never presented as current, for EVERY tracked indicator (generic fix, not dow_jones-only). New `FIX-DOWJONES-STALE-WRONG-VALUE.test.ts` (15 tests: G-1..G-4 plausibility band accept/reject incl. the literal 23750/10604/76848 phantom values, G-5/G-6 news-mining retirement + sibling-pattern regression, G-7 live ^DJI fetch parsing, G-8..G-10 store fail-closed + dedup + null no-op, G-11/G-12 `[STALE]` tag presence/absence) + extended `1920c-commodity-tracker-refresh-job.test.ts` (TC-8..TC-10, Block-3 isolation + fail-closed rejection path). ## [Developer] 2026-07-25 — FIX-COLDEVICT-DONE-LANE-TRIGGER-ACTION-AXIS-NOOP (cross-service, developer): three coupled defects in `scripts/orch-cold-evict.sh`/`scripts/agents-flow/dev-team-tick-preflight.sh` Step 5.5. (A) `done[]` sort was a plain STRING sort on `created_at` — a poison value (`"unknown"`, 10 of 16 live rows) string-sorted above every ISO date and ranked as newest, permanently un-evictable; fixed to sort on parsed epoch (`try fromdateiso8601 catch 0`), matching the age-gate's own malformed-value convention. (B) the Step 5.5 trigger was a hand-rolled COUNT-only proxy (`done_n>10`) on a different axis than the script's COUNT-AND-AGE eviction gate, so the trigger could be permanently true while the action stayed a permanent no-op, and the proxy had no `signal_queue` term even though the script always sweeps it; fixed by having the trigger ask the script itself via `--dry-run` + a `Byte reduction:` parse (`_step55_would_evict`) — trigger and action are now the same computation, restoring the signal_queue disjunct for free. (C) `[.array // [] | .[].id // ""]` misreported a genuinely empty `done_verified[]`/`signal_queue.archive[]` as 1 evicted item (jq `//` fires on zero-output, not just null) — fixed by moving `[]` outside the `// []`. New regression coverage: `scripts/test/orch-cold-evict-tests.sh` TEST 7 (poison-sort + phantom-counter, real fixture) and `scripts/agents-flow/dev-team-tick-preflight.test.sh` T33-T35 (axis-independence + real unstubbed both-directions dry-run). Live data cleanup: all 10 malformed `done[]` rows reached a terminal state (6 auto-evicted by a concurrent dev-team tick mid-fix, using the corrected on-disk sort; the remaining 4 evicted via a one-time `KEEP_RECENT_DONE=6` run — the 6 genuinely-recent done rows were the negative control and are untouched). `docs/agents/dev-team/flow/post-cycle.md` § Step 4.2 (CANON spec) updated to match. QA next.

## [Developer] 2026-07-28 — FIX-COMMIT-PATH-PEER-INDEX-SWEEP-GUARD-HOOK (scripts/, developer, P0, explicit supervised dispatch): Layer-0 universal backstop for the recurring bare-commit sweep bug (4 confirmed occurrences, most recently 2026-07-28T19:5xZ, commit `09ae11440` — swept 5 of PO's own staged paths into a dev-mcp-server-attributed commit; the already-shipped Layer-1 skills fix did NOT prevent this because the victim never staged anything itself). New `scripts/git-hooks/pre-commit` — implements architecture brief `docs/architecture-briefs/2026-07-21-commit-path-peer-index-sweep-guard.md` §4.1 exactly: `$GIT_INDEX_FILE` basename discriminator (`index`/`index.lock`=BARE, race-free because the hook runs inside git's own index.lock transaction; `next-index-<pid>.lock`=pathspec-SCOPED, structurally immune, git resolves it into a private scratch index before invoking the hook). WARN-by-default fleet-wide (never blocks unless a caller opts its own commit into `GIT_SWEEP_GUARD_MODE=reject`, only sensible once that site's own commit line is pathspec-scoped) — 3-channel observability, all non-silent: stderr banner, `.git/sweep-guard.log`, best-effort `docs/signals/*.json` bug-escalation write (bash+jq only, zero MCP/network dependency, exact live schema match verified against `docs/signals/processed/news-scout-20260724T040000Z.json`). Internal errors (unrecognized `$GIT_INDEX_FILE` shape, or a `git diff --cached` failure) fail OPEN + LOUD, never silently swallowed — mirrors commit-mutex's own C-2/C-2b precedent. Structurally binds the INV-GATEWAY-1-exempt population (dev-\*/qa/ba/pm/architect) since a git hook sits beneath any MCP-bound skill and cannot be opted out the way `commit-mutex` can. New companion `scripts/git-hooks/post-commit` (optional SHA-correlation enrichment, non-blocking). `scripts/git-hooks/install.sh` extended (`for hook in pre-push pre-commit post-commit`) so a fresh clone / `.git` rebuild re-installs all three. New permanent regression suite `scripts/git-hooks/pre-commit.test.sh` (6/6 PASS, disposable scratch repos only, never the live repo) covering bare-sweeps-foreign, pathspec-excludes-foreign-even-after-`add -A`, the directory/dot-pathspec loophole documented as a known non-goal (brief §2.3), rebase-replay-no-false-warn, reject-mode-blocks-and-preserves-index, and UNKNOWN-shape fail-open+loud. Re-ran `scripts/audits/verify-commit-sweep-discriminator.sh` before implementing (VERDICT PASS, git 2.49.0, premise still holds) and again after (still PASS). **Live verification gate reproduced in a disposable scratch clone of the real repo** (not asserted from hook source prose): actor A stages a file, actor B bare-commits while A's content sits in the shared index — under WARN (shipped default) the sweep is loudly flagged and the commit lands (intentional day-1 disposition per architect's WARN-by-default ruling, real command output captured); under opt-in `GIT_SWEEP_GUARD_MODE=reject` the commit is rejected (exit 1), index left untouched, A's content fully recoverable, B's fix is a single pathspec-scoped retry — demonstrated live end-to-end. `shellcheck` clean on all 4 new/modified files. `docs/policies/dev-standards.md` § Script Persistence updated with the CANONICAL pointer. Parent `FIX-COMMIT-PATH-PEER-INDEX-SWEEP-GUARD` remains BLOCKED pending PO closeout (not closed here — PO's call). QA next.

STRUCTURAL fix first (per the row's explicit "do not stop at the split alone" instruction): `docs/agents/alert-commander/flow/stage-dispatch-log.md` § 5 now writes one top-level `## c<NNN> · <ISO-timestamp> (slot=..., tick=...)` section per cycle, routed through the shared `.claude/skills/notebook-write/SKILL.md` APPEND-class contract (AC-2/AC-3/AC-4), template capped ≤700B/≤8L with full tool-call narration redirected to `log_agent_work`'s context field (historical entries averaged ~4145B/632B-per-line — structure alone would not have fit 3 retained sections under the byte cap). ONE-OFF remediation second: the pre-fix blob archived verbatim → `docs/agent-memory/notebooks/archive/alert-commander-archive-20260729.md` (line-cap-exempt per `context-bloat-backstop.test.sh` T1); live notebook reset to a 7L/1099B preamble, 0 `## ` sections (AC-4 blank-state — next real cycle lands as `## c1`). Cleared the stale `depends: [FIX-NOTEBOOK-PRUNER-LINE-ONLY-SETPOINT-BYTE-CAP-NEVER-CONVERGES]` edge per PO ruling (verified non-causal at source against that row's own board note — this file's single-section shape defeats the sibling pruner fix under either axis regardless of the sibling's own verification state). Updated `docs/evals/alert-commander.eval.json` AC-11, which had encoded the pre-fix `### Alert Cycle` heading as the required contract (would have certified a regression). Also net-shrank `stage-dispatch-log.md` from 129L to 117L (now under the 120L `flow-file` class cap; the file's byte count, 9624B pre-edit, was already over the 7200B byte-cap before this task touched it — pre-existing, out of this row's scope, Steps 1-4 dispatch/claim-truth-gate logic untouched). No live MCP tools in this session's grant (`Read/Edit/Write/Glob/Grep/Bash` only) — cannot execute a real alert-commander cycle to observe a live write; verified by design/simulation (3-cycle budget ≈3199B/22L, wide margin under both caps) and direct inspection of the already-shipped `notebook-auto-prune.sh` timestamp-parsing logic (commit `b42f3fa3a`). ## [Developer] 2026-07-29 — FIX-AUDITOR-MEMACK-HEADROOM-FLOOR-AND-DEAD-TRACKEDBY (cross-service, developer, P1, sprint INFRA-AUDIT, BOUNDED-1 idle-capacity auto-pickup): `auditor-tier1-probe.sh`'s memory-ACK ledger (`docs/data/auditor-launchd-ack.json` `.acked_memory[]`/`.acked[]`) had two stacked defects — `tracked_by` was read by nothing anywhere in the script, so an ACK could never expire even past its tracked fix reaching DONE_VERIFIED, and the mem-creep ACK predicate was a bare `pct>=85` test with no absolute-headroom floor, so 85.01% and 99.99% suppressed identically. PO-measured live consequence: rag-service sat ACK-suppressed at 97.11% of its 768 MiB cap (22.2 MiB free, falling to 11.4 MiB 15 minutes later) while Tier-1 reported ALL_GREEN. Fix, 3 parts. (a) New `MEM_FLOOR_MIB=40` constant + `_mem_headroom_mib()` computing absolute free MiB from values the check already pulls (`mem_cap*(100-pct)/100`, no extra docker call) — an ACK'd container below the floor no longer suppresses, verdict FAILS naming the container's percentage AND its MiB-free. Calibrated at 2x the ~20 MiB measured rag-service `compact()`-failure-path optimize() burst (`FIX-RAG-SERVICE-CLEAN-EXIT-RESTART-LOOP` root_cause), NOT the cap size, per PO's explicit calibration note; pdf-extractor's own burst profile (`FIX-PDFX-PARENT-PROCESS-MEMORY-BURST-HEADROOM`) is qualitatively larger but still undiagnosed/BACKLOG with no acked_memory entry today, so it doesn't constrain this global constant yet. (b) New shared `_task_status_in_orch_state()` resolves a `tracked_by` id against EVERY `task_board` lane (8 array lanes + `active_sprints[]`/`closed_sprints[]` nested tasks) — absent from every lane, or resolved to DONE_VERIFIED, is a STALE ACK and refuses to suppress; fail-loud (a distinct rc=2) on an unreadable/unparseable orch-state.json, never `2>/dev/null || true`. (c) Applied identically to the `acked[]` launchd arm (`_launchd_label_acked`, same dead-field defect, 3 live entries), per-entry — one sibling ack going stale doesn't disable a still-live one. Caught + fixed a real locale bug in my own new code along the way: `awk '%.1f'` prints comma-decimal under this box's `fr_FR.UTF-8` locale, the exact class already documented in `scripts/audits/verify-a30-mcp-memory-reclamation.sh`'s LOCALE PIN comment — pinned `LC_ALL=C LC_NUMERIC=C` scoped to the individual awk calls (not a global `export`, since this script is sourced by its own test harness). New `auditor-tier1-probe.test.sh` T44-T53 (26 assertions, 167/167 total, was 141/141): floor boundary either side of 40 MiB, DONE_VERIFIED + ABSENT staleness on both ack arms, missing-file + malformed-JSON orch-state fail-loud on both arms, a mixed live+stale sibling case. Added an `ORCH_STATE_PATH` test seam (mirrors `SYSTEM_MAP_PATH`/`LAUNCHD_ACK_PATH`) — discovered it was MISSING first: the pre-existing T36/T40 cases were silently resolving `tracked_by` against the REAL live repo board, passing only because those 3 specific ids happen to be non-terminal today; a future DONE_VERIFIED on any of them would have silently broken these tests with zero test-file change. RED proved via `git stash` (old script + new test file): crashes `unbound variable: ORCH_STATE` — the old script structurally cannot satisfy the new contract. `shellcheck` clean (2 pre-existing warnings only, unchanged from before this task). **AC6 raw evidence, real fleet, not a stub:** ran the actual script against the live repo (`date -u` 2026-07-29T12:09:09Z) — `docker inspect`/`docker stats` snapshot captured alongside the probe's own JSON output, verdict FAILURE, detail `rag-service(99.22%, 6.0MiB-free, BELOW-FLOOR(floor=40MiB))`. **Operational consequence, flagged not fixed:** shipping this flips the LIVE rag-service ACK from suppressed to FAILURE immediately (real headroom is ~6-11 MiB right now, worsening) — this is the fix's INTENDED, DESIGNED outcome (its own AC2), not a regression; the whole point of the row was that the old detector could not distinguish this from a benign steady-state. Updated `docs/data/auditor-launchd-ack.json`'s `_comment`/`_comment_acked_memory` (staleness rule now code-enforced, was prose-only) and added a `floor_enforcement_20260729` field on the rag-service entry documenting the live consequence for whoever next reads the ledger or watches Tier-1 go red. Did NOT touch rag-service's actual memory usage (`FU-RAG-DEPLOY-MEMORY`/`FIX-RAG-SERVICE-CLEAN-EXIT-RESTART-LOOP`, explicit `does_not_cover`) or widen the floor/ACK to silence the new FAILURE — that would recreate the exact blind suppression this row exists to close; routed to PO if the resulting cron churn needs a capacity decision. No `apps/` files touched (zone=`cross-service/`, pure shell); `bun test`/`tsc` structurally N/A. QA next.

## [Developer] 2026-07-29 — FACTORY-GUARD-CI-SIZELINT-IMPL (cross-service, developer, epic FACTORY-MAINTAINABILITY-2026-06): implemented the size-lint-justification CI guardrail decomposed by architect brief `docs/architecture-briefs/2026-07-24-factory-guard-ci-size-lint-justification.md` — the code-plane sibling to `scripts/agents-flow/context-bloat-backstop.sh` (that hook's own SSOT explicitly excludes "Code and data JSON"). New `scripts/audits/size-lint-justification.sh` walks `apps/**/*.ts|*.py|*.go` + `packages/**/*.ts` (git-tracked + untracked-but-not-gitignored, via `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`, excl. tests/vendor/`.d.ts`) in two modes: `--check` (CI, exit 0/1, no writes) and `--update` (regenerates `docs/data/size-lint-baseline.json` from live repo state, prune-eligible entries drop automatically since it's a wholesale regen not a merge). Baseline/ratchet design per brief §2: file ≤120L or carries a CURRENT `size-justification:` header → PASS; file >120L, grandfathered in baseline AND within ±10%/min-5L tolerance (mirrors the hook's own idiom) → PASS; grown past tolerance or a brand-new unjustified offender → FAIL. **Live-verify catch:** discovered mid-implementation that a large share of real headers declare an *approximate* count (`~180L`, not a bare `180L`) — the brief's own worked examples didn't show this, but grepping live headers did; the number-extraction regex now accepts an optional `~`, without which ~10 legitimately-justified files would have false-failed. Ran `--update` once to generate the initial baseline: 666 unjustified offenders as of 2026-07-29 (brief's 2026-07-24 count of 733 had already drifted down via other FACTORY-* split work in the intervening 5 days, as flagged as expected in the board note). Wired `size-lint` job into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (ubuntu-latest, checkout-only, no toolchain). Added CANONICAL pointer in `docs/policies/dev-standards.md` § Script Persistence. New `scripts/audits/size-lint-justification.test.sh` — 6/6 PASS, covering all 4 DoD cases (live-repo `--check` exits 0 with the fresh baseline; synthetic new unjustified file fails; synthetic baseline file grown past tolerance fails; a shrunk file AND a newly-justified file are both dropped by `--update`) plus 2 bonus controls (baseline-within-tolerance positive control; `--update` idempotency excl. timestamp, manually verified). All fixtures live in a disposable untracked `apps/mcp-server/src/__size_lint_fixtures__/` dir scoped via a new `SIZE_LINT_INCLUDE_OVERRIDE`/`SIZE_LINT_BASELINE_OVERRIDE` env-var seam (mirrors `gen-tools-index.sh`'s `*_OVERRIDE` idiom) — never touches the real repo tree or the real baseline file; cleaned up on exit. No `apps/mcp-server/src/` files touched — `bun test`/`tsc` structurally N/A. QA next.

## [Developer] 2026-07-29 — FACTORY-GUARD-CI-METRICMASK-IMPL (cross-service, developer, epic FACTORY-MAINTAINABILITY-2026-06, BOUNDED-1 idle-capacity auto-pickup): implemented the metric-mask-lint CI guardrail decomposed by architect brief `docs/architecture-briefs/2026-07-24-factory-guard-ci-metric-mask-lint.md`. New `scripts/audits/metric-mask-lint.sh` (`--check` only, zero-tolerance — NOT baseline/ratchet like the size-lint sibling, live debt was small enough to fix in the same task) scans `apps/**/*.ts|*.py|*.go` + `packages/**/*.ts` for a silent `?? <non-zero-literal>` / `|| <non-zero-literal>` / Python `or <non-zero-literal>` / destructuring-or-param-default fallback on an identifier matching `/confidence|score|impact|magnitude|probability/i` (the "confidence_score=50" fabrication class); `0`/`0.0`/`null` always allowed; comment-only keyword mentions never scanned as code; inline `metric-mask-allow: <reason>` on the same/preceding line suppresses. Fixed the 3 live `cascadeEngine.ts:356,375,394` `seedEntry.confidence ?? 0.6` masks feeding `detectMsciInclusion`/`Watchlist`/`Exclusion` — changed to `?? 0`, which is behaviorally IDENTICAL to the old value for every real input (both sit below the detector's 0.7 credibility floor → reject), zero-risk per the repo-wide `confidence ?? 0` idiom (15+ existing sites). Fixed `marketSentimentCalculator.ts:174` `row.impact_score ?? 1.0` — since `impact_score` is genuinely nullable in `rag_analyses` (unlike cascadeEngine's always-populated `confidence`), `?? 0` would have fabricated a "zero impact" weight while still counting the row's confidence in the divide-by-zero denominator; the honest fix excludes the row from the weighted average entirely, mirroring the function's own pre-existing confidence-null guard. Annotated `watchlist.ts:198` (`thresholds?.impactScore ?? 7`, genuine user-configurable alert threshold) with `metric-mask-allow:`. **Verify-live catch:** a live dry-run of the built lint against the real repo (before landing any fix) surfaced a 5th real hit beyond the brief's stated "4 real bugs + 1 legit default" — `brokerCredibilityTools.ts:51` `baseConfidence = 1` (a zod `.default(1)`-backed, documented, caller-facing input default the brief's own manual sweep missed) — annotated it too with `metric-mask-allow:` rather than narrowing the detector's real-bug-catching power to dodge it. Wired `metric-mask-lint` job into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (ubuntu-latest, checkout-only, no toolchain). CANONICAL pointer added to `dev-standards.md` § Script Persistence. New `scripts/audits/metric-mask-lint.test.sh` — 10/10 PASS (the 4 DoD cases: live-repo `--check` exits 0; synthetic `?? 50`-style mask fails; `?? 0`/`?? null` pass; an annotated line passes — plus a negative-control positive-control pair and 3 bonus shape controls for `||`/python-`or`/destructuring-default). Updated the one existing test that encoded the old masked behavior (`P0-4-market-sentiment-index.test.ts` "uses impact_score=1.0 default when null" → rewritten to assert exclusion, plus a new co-dated-row test proving only the null-impact row is dropped) and added a new regression test in `FIX-1279-msci-bullish.test.ts` proving the absent-confidence case still produces zero MSCI cascade entries post-fix (same outcome as the old 0.6 default). RAW-verified no scoring-behavior regression: all directly-relevant suites 100% green (FIX-1279-msci-bullish 14/14, P0-4-market-sentiment-index 37/37, 1279a+247+FIX-1268+1224 cascade files 31/31, 915-broker-credibility 22/22, 082+220-watchlist 25/25), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `metric-mask-lint.sh --check` exits 0 on the post-fix live repo, shellcheck clean on both new scripts. Full `bun test`: 14904-14907 pass / 40 skip / 60-63 fail / 15007 total (2 runs, slight count drift between runs) — every failure is pre-existing order-dependent full-suite pollution tracked by the still-BACKLOG board row `FIX-MCP-SUITE-HEALTH-BASELINE` ("standing bun-test baseline... order-dependent... loop-pollution"), none touching any file this task changed; confirmed directly (not just asserted) by re-running 3 sampled "failing" files in isolation — 32/32 pass standalone. New `scripts/audits/shared-package-import-check.sh` (`--check`/`--update`, baseline/ratchet like `size-lint-justification.sh` — NOT zero-tolerance, different justifying axis: the debt's fix is a domain keep-or-cut decision explicitly owned by the separate, larger, still-BACKLOG `FACTORY-SHARED-wire-or-prune-shared-packages` task, not this CI-tooling child task's to make). Check 1 (blocking): for every `packages/*/package.json` declaring a `@vn-market/`-scoped `name`, grep `apps/**`+`packages/**` (own package dir excluded) for a real import specifier (`from '@vn-market/<pkg>'`/`require('@vn-market/<pkg>')`) or `package.json` dependency entry — >=1 real importer always PASSes regardless of baseline membership; 0 importers + baseline-listed PASSes (prints `BASELINE: <pkg> — tracked by FACTORY-SHARED-wire-or-prune-shared-packages`); 0 importers + NOT baseline-listed FAILs, blocking a brand-new phantom package (the `packages/primitives/technical-analysis` shape, already pruned once) from landing again without a human decision. Deliberately scoped the importer search to `apps/**`+`packages/**` only, NOT `docs/**` — this repo's own architecture briefs discuss these package names by name in prose, and scanning docs/ would let that discussion text itself be misread as a "real importer," permanently masking the debt. Live debt at design time: 3/3 `packages/shared-*` packages 100% orphaned (`shared-types`/`shared-config`/`shared-db`, live-verified zero real hits) — seeded into `docs/data/shared-package-import-baseline.json` via `--update` in the same task that shipped the gate. Check 2 (ADVISORY ONLY, never fails `--check`): scans top-level `export interface|type|const|function <Name>` in `packages/shared-*/index.ts`, prints an `ADVISORY:` line for every symbol name independently re-exported anywhere under `apps/**/*.ts` (excl. tests/vendor) — today's live hits go beyond the brief's own cited examples (`Alert`/`Signal`/`McpConfig`): also `loadMcpConfig`/`ExtractPDFRequest`/`ExtractPDFResponse`/`ComputeTARequest`/`ComputeTAResponse`/`SearchRequest`/`SearchResult`/`ServiceHealth` — the brief's "e.g." wording was explicitly non-exhaustive, kept the general scan since it's advisory-only (extra lines are strictly more informative, never break `--check`). Full AST structural diffing to make check 2 blocking is explicitly deferred (no TS AST tool is wired into any bash-only audit script in this repo; a regex field-diff would false-positive on reorder/JSDoc/optional-marker churn — same reasoning as the dead-code-gate/no-hardcode siblings' own deferrals). **Verify-live catch:** first draft's check-1/check-2 loops spawned one `grep` subprocess PER candidate file PER package/symbol (~7K+~34K forks at this repo's file count) — measured via a hung background run (>2min, still running when checked) before trusting it, not a passive read. Root-cause fixed: batched every candidate file array into ONE `grep -l ... -- "${files[@]}"` call per package/symbol (O(1) forks regardless of corpus size) — re-measured 7s standalone / 14s full smoke suite. Wired `shared-package-import-check` job into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (ubuntu-latest, checkout-only, no toolchain). CANONICAL pointer added to `dev-standards.md` § Script Persistence. New `scripts/audits/shared-package-import-check.test.sh` — 4/4 PASS covering all 4 DoD cases (a baseline-listed zero-importer fixture package passes; a synthetic new zero-importer package NOT in baseline fails; a synthetic package with a real importer passes even though ALSO still baseline-listed; the Alert/Signal/McpConfig advisory lines emit on the live repo without affecting the green exit code). Zero edits to `packages/shared-*/` contents, zero new consumer wiring, zero package deletion, zero field reconciliation — all explicitly out of scope, reserved for `FACTORY-SHARED-wire-or-prune-shared-packages` (still BACKLOG). No `apps/` source touched (zone=`cross-service/`, pure bash+yaml+md+json) — `bun test`/`tsc` structurally N/A. `shellcheck` clean (1 benign SC2329 info on the trap-invoked `cleanup()`, same pattern as `size-lint-justification.test.sh`'s own). No MCP/gateway tool grant in this session ("local subagents gateway blind") — flipped the board row via `scripts/orch-apply.sh` directly; coordinating dev-team session must release `task:FACTORY-GUARD-CI-SHAREDPKG-IMPL`. ## [Developer] 2026-07-30 — FIX-AUDITOR-T1-PREGATE-MEMCREEP-SINGLE-POINT-SAMPLE (cross-service, developer, BOUNDED-1 idle-capacity auto-pickup): fixed `_check_mem_creep()` in `scripts/agents-flow/auditor-tier1-probe.sh` — the shell pre-gate deciding SKIP-SPAWN/SPAWN for the system-auditor subagent took exactly ONE `docker stats` sample per container per invocation and gated ALL_GREEN/FAILURE off that single reading; a live re-run ~5.5min after a cited ALL_GREEN result flipped to FAILURE (pdf-extractor 99.91% MemPerc), a transient peak the single-point sample missed entirely. New `MEM_CREEP_SAMPLES` (>=2, default 2, clamped) / `MEM_CREEP_SAMPLE_INTERVAL_SEC` (default 2s) top-of-script knobs (test seams); the per-container check now loops N samples and gates off the WORST (max) parsed pct observed — a breach in ANY one sample forces FAILURE for that container even if another sample in the same short window reads comfortably GREEN; any single sample's stats-unavailable/unparseable still breaches (unchanged fail-loud). Zero changes to `run_probe()`'s call site, the acked_memory[]/headroom-floor/tracked_by-liveness ledger arm, or the `{verdict,detail,last_healthy_at}` output schema — same stdout message shape and rc=0|1 contract as before. DISTINCT mechanism from `FIX-AUDITOR-TIER1-A30-MEM-SINGLE-CONTAINER-SCOPE` (that fix widened WHICH containers are looked at; this fix widens HOW MANY TIMES each is sampled per invocation) and from `FIX-AUDITOR-A30-VMHWM-VETO-TAUTOLOGY-FALSE-NEGATIVE` (unrelated mcp-server VmHWM veto) — neither touched. Verified `docs/agents/system-auditor/probe.sh` (the separate evidence-collector the LLM subagent runs when actually spawned) is a DIFFERENT script/mechanism, single-container-scoped, already carrying its own distinct 2-point/30-min-delta A-30 override — out of this task's explicit scope, untouched. New regression suite T54-T58 in `auditor-tier1-probe.test.sh`: T54 reproduces the exact live incident (sample1=70.00% GREEN, sample2=99.91% FAIL -> verdict FAILURE, worst-pct named, not the first-sampled one); T55 proves worst-of-window (not last-sample) via reversed order; T56/T57/T58 prove the loop actually calls `docker stats` N times via a file-based call-count assertion (in-memory associative-array counter was tried first and discarded — silently reset across the `$(...)` subshell fork `docker stats` is invoked through) and that MEM_CREEP_SAMPLES<2 is defensively clamped back to the AC's N>=2 floor. Full suite 181/181 PASS (167 pre-existing byte-identical + 14 new checks), ~12s runtime (MEM_CREEP_SAMPLE_INTERVAL_SEC exported =0 for the whole test file so the new inter-sample sleep never slows any case). `shellcheck` clean (2 pre-existing unrelated warnings only). Decision journal named per the settled precedent for this exact task family (sprint-FIX-AUDITOR-TIER1-A30-MEM-SINGLE-CONTAINER-SCOPE-developer.md/sprint-FIX-AUDITOR-TIER1-PROBE-ACKED-LAUNCHD-DEATH-SUPPRESSION-developer.md both use TASK_ID as SPRINT_ID) rather than the mechanically-resolved but unrelated `COWORK-GUARANTEED-SLOT-CATCHUP` active sprint (orch-state.json currently carries many concurrently-`active` `sprint_goal.entries[]`, a separate governance debt item, not fixed here). ## [Developer] 2026-07-31 — FIX-SWEEPGUARD-ESCALATION-RETROACTIVE-COUNTER-AND-SESSION-SCOPED-ACTOR (cross-service, developer, P1, BOUNDED-1 idle-capacity auto-pickup, direct follow-up to the row above): the escalation actuator shipped in `eac71308e` had two live defects the moment it landed — (D1) `prior_warns` counted the ENTIRE unwindowed historical `.git/sweep-guard.log`, so 2 already-live sessions (`64c7c677-...` at 70, `ad265f86-...` at 6) were instantly over threshold=3 with no deploy baseline, a de-facto instant fleet-wide reject flip the owning brief had deliberately staged as a 24h-observation-gated Phase 2; (D2) `actor=$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` is shared by every subagent spawned from one router session, so the actuator converges SESSIONS not offending agents — the exact fleet-wide behavior the brief said it was avoiding. **AC-1 fix:** self-initializing per-clone deploy-baseline marker (`.git/sweep-guard.escalation-baseline`, a UTC timestamp created once on the first post-deploy hook invocation) — `prior_warns` now only counts BARE log lines timestamped at/after that floor via lexicographic string compare on the fixed `date -u` ISO8601 shape (no date parsing, no BSD/GNU `date -d` portability trap); the live, append-only `.git/sweep-guard.log` was never truncated. Re-ran the hook's OWN real code (not a reimplementation) against a byte-copy of the UNMODIFIED live log in a disposable scratch repo: both flagged actors now compute `prior_warns=0` (was 70 / 6 under the old unwindowed predicate), both WARN-only, neither escalated. **AC-2:** verified empirically (not assumed) that no per-agent identifier narrower than `$CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` is reachable inside a git hook subprocess — `CLAUDE_CODE_BRIDGE_SESSION_ID` is coarser (spans many different coordination sessions), `CLAUDE_PID` is the top-level host process (also constant fleet-wide within a session tree), `$$` changes every invocation. Renamed the concept "per-actor" → "per-session" throughout the hook's comments (wire-format `actor=` field kept unchanged for log/signal continuity); `threshold=3` kept unchanged (the brief's own "14 warns/4 sessions" math was already a per-session average, not per-agent — D2 was a mislabel, not a miscalibration) but now explicitly documented as a POOLED per-coordination-session budget, flagged for the 24h observation window to validate against real 8+-agent-wide sessions. **AC-3:** rollback (`GIT_SWEEP_GUARD_ESCALATE_THRESHOLD=0`) and the restarted 24h observation window (counts from this fix's own deploy timestamp, not `eac71308e`'s — the pre-fix window is discarded as contaminated) now documented in `docs/policies/dev-standards.md` CANONICAL: same-session escalation actuator, an always-loaded policy doc an agent reads before ever committing, not only reactively via the stderr block message. **AC-4/AC-5:** new T10 in `pre-commit.test.sh` — a scratch repo seeded with a frozen BYTE-COPY of the real live `.git/sweep-guard.log` (`scripts/git-hooks/fixtures/sweep-guard-live-snapshot-2026-07-31.log`, not a fresh empty log) proves the seeded actor's first post-deploy commit is NOT blocked despite 70 pre-existing over-threshold lines, and that escalation still fires normally afterward off the post-baseline count only. Full suite 10/10 PASS. Scope fence honored — did not touch the parent row (still REVIEW/qa) or any other sweep-guard family row; `docs/agents/po/flow/triage-signals.md` and `.git/sweep-guard.log` read-only per task spec. No `apps/` TS/Go source touched (zone `cross-service/`, pure bash+md) — `bun test`/`tsc` structurally N/A. QA next.

## [Developer] 2026-08-01 — FIX-SWEEPGUARD-SAMEFILE-DETECTOR-UNSTAGED-PATH-FALSE-POSITIVE (cross-service, developer, P1, BOUNDED-1 idle-capacity auto-pickup): `_detect_samefile_pathspec_only_divergence()` false-positived on every ordinary tracked-file pathspec commit that was never `git add`'d first (e.g. every `scripts/orch-apply.sh` atomic-rename write followed by `git commit -- <path>`, which never stages via `git add`) — root cause: `git rev-parse ":$f"` against the REAL shared index returns the HEAD blob for ANY tracked path and never returns empty, so the existing `[ -z "$real_blob" ] && continue` fail-open branch was dead code for the entire population it claimed to protect, making an unstaged tracked file indistinguishable from a peer-staged conflicting hunk. Landed all 5 fixes from `po_amendment_20260807T0610Z`: (a) RestartCount/StartedAt/OOMKilled/ExitCode/FinishedAt now re-read AFTER the sample loop too, any RestartCount/StartedAt delta forces `ESCALATE` naming both before/after values; (b) the two percentage escalation branches no longer hard-gate on `dips==0` — `min_pct>93` is ungated, and the old single-sample `max_pct>97` branch (structurally unsafe once ungated — a lone benign ~97.8% peak would false-positive) was replaced with `median_pct>97`, a jitter-tolerant sustained-peak check; (c) any single-step drop >40pp is now classified as a `discontinuity` (own counter, own ESCALATE branch), never counted in `reclamation_dips`; (d) `ExitCode==0` + a `FinishedAt` delta between before/after is an accepted death signature alongside `OOMKilled==true`; (e) the VmHWM-vs-VmRSS tautology narrative is gone — VmHWM is now compared against the cgroup memory limit (`HostConfig.Memory`) and its own prior value across the window (advancing + pinned at >=90% of cap = `ESCALATE`), never against VmRSS. Refactored the script into a sourceable `run_a30_reclamation_check()` function (BASH_SOURCE guard, same idiom as `db-integrity-mount-drift-check.sh`/`auditor-tier1-probe.sh`) purely so it could be tested — zero behavior change from the refactor alone, confirmed by hand-replaying the pre-fix verdict chain against the same 12-sample death series before adding the 5 fixes (FOLD, matching the live incident). New `scripts/audits/verify-a30-mcp-memory-reclamation.test.sh`, 13/13 pass: T1 is the board's own mandatory `verification_gate` (exact 2026-08-07T05:56Z 12-sample series + RestartCount 0->1 -> `ESCALATE`, confirmed); T2/T3/T6b/T8/T9 are negative controls (benign sawtooth, single-spike-then-reclaim, stale FinishedAt, VmHWM-pinned-not-advancing, VmHWM-advancing-not-pinned) proving the fix did not become a new false-positive generator; T4/T5/T6/T7/T10/T11 exercise each of fix (a)-(e) independently. Also absorbs the phantom `FIX-AUDITOR-A30-VMHWM-VETO-TAUTOLOGY-FALSE-NEGATIVE` dependency (never minted in any lane/archive — fix (e) covers its scope). Doc-consumer sync (the JSON output contract changed — old field names like `state.oom_killed`/`vm.vmhwm_kb` and 2 of 3 escalate-reason substrings no longer exist): `docs/agents/system-auditor/flow/tier1-probe.md` § A-30 override (parsed-field list + reason->severity mapping widened from 3 to 6 CRITICAL substrings, corrected the stale "not consumed by any verdict mapping" VmHWM claim and the dangling phantom-row citation), `docs/agents/cowork-team/flow/pressure-read.md` (escalate-path summary), `docs/agents/system-auditor/probe.sh` (comment only). No `apps/` TS touched (pure bash under `scripts/audits/`) — `bun test`/`tsc` structurally N/A; verified live against the real running `vn-market-intelligence-mcp-mcp-server-1` container (3 probes/1s) and confirmed `bash -n` + full test suite pass under both the PATH bash and macOS system `/bin/bash` 3.2.57. QA next.

Live `orch-state.json` never touched by any test (isolated mkdtemp harnesses only), confirmed via post-session `orch-validate.mjs` PASS. ## [Developer] 2026-08-09 — TASK_RUNIDLE-1-AUDIT (cross-service/dev-flow/, developer, high, Tier 1 of the `FIX-RUNIDLE-PREDICATE-D-ACTIVE-SPRINTS-PERMANENT-FLOOR` decomposition, audit-only): audited why `active_sprints[]` never reaches zero, blocking `_step5_idle_check()` predicate (d) permanently. Traced the ADD side (no canonical "open sprint" flow — every live sprint was opened by a bespoke one-off `scripts/po-s*.jq` script or a hand-authored commit, `sprint-kickoff.md` only writes `sprint_goal.entries[]`) and the REMOVE side (PM's reactive UC-DTL-P9 status-flip `scripts/pm-closeout-head-idle.jq`, `task-archive.md`'s inline nested-tasks-all-terminal eviction predicate, `orch-cold-evict.sh`'s canonical sprint's-own-status-terminal predicate — the two REMOVE predicates disagree, and 20 real historical closures confirm the mechanism works per-sprint, just not fast/broadly enough). Live-audited all 8 `active_sprints[]`: 2 (`SPRINT-CCATO-TRUTHGATE-MCP-NATIVE`, `SYSREMAKE-P2-STRUCTURAL-REMAKE-ROUTE`) are both stale (23d, one carrying a malformed double-`Z` timestamp) and childless — they use a `subtasks: [id,...]` string-array schema instead of nested `tasks[]`, and all their subtask ids are dangling (zero board-row matches anywhere), so PM's only closeout trigger (reactive, QA-Done-coupled) can structurally never fire for them. Root cause = 2 structural gaps: (1) steady-state concurrent-sprint flow keeps the array populated since removal requires 100%-nested-task completion and nothing caps WIP sprint count; (2) the orphaned SPRINT-S shape has no event that ever re-examines its closeout eligibility. Checked both PO-guardrail duplicate candidates: `SPIKE-SATURATED-COUNT-THRESHOLD-GATES-SWEEP` is inventory-only by its own stated scope (not a duplicate — this line is the fix row its scope defers to); `FIX-SPRINT-REGISTRY-DANGLING-IDS-BREAK-SIGNOFF-AND-JOURNAL-ARCHIVE` is the mirror problem (sprint ids referenced with no registry object) — related, not duplicate. Findings + Task 2/3 recommendations in `docs/architecture-briefs/2026-08-09-active-sprints-accumulator-gap.md`. No code changes (audit-only per handoff); no new `active_sprints[]`/`sprint_goal.entries[]` row minted for this task's own work (PO guardrail 1). Board row `backlog[]` → `review[]`, `next_agent=pm`. Feeds `TASK_RUNIDLE-2-REDESIGN` + `TASK_RUNIDLE-3-STALENESS` (both `depends_on` this row).

## [Developer] 2026-08-14 — FIX-NSO-TS-KEY-COMMIT-SHA-DIGITS-PARSED-AS-DATE (cross-service/, developer, P0 S, BOUNDED-1 idle-capacity auto-pickup): fixed live P0 data-loss defect in `scripts/agents-flow/lib/notebook-section-direction.sh`'s `nso_ts_key()` — the old `grep -oE <date-regex> | tail -1` treated a heading's ISO time-of-day suffix as OPTIONAL, so an all-numeric git short-SHA cited later in a `## cycle-N · <ISO ts> · ... commit \`<sha>\`` heading (real live convention, `docs/agent-memory/notebooks/qa.md`) matched the same pattern as the real timestamp, and `tail -1` always preferred the LAST match on the line — the SHA, not the timestamp — silently misranking section age and causing `notebook-auto-prune.sh`'s drop-oldest loop to delete the wrong section (confirmed live: destroyed `TASK-COWORK-MUTEX-001`'s QA CHANGES_REQUESTED review record with zero signal). Fix: two-tier extraction — Tier 1 requires the literal `T..Z` suffix (`head -1`, first match; hex SHAs can never satisfy this, no T/Z chars in 0-9a-f) with Tier 2 fallback (bare date-only convention) now boundary-guarded (`(^|[^0-9])...([^0-9]|$)`) so a truncated digit-run prefix of a longer SHA can never match either. New RED-first regression `T11` added to `scripts/agents-flow/notebook-auto-prune.test.sh` reproducing the exact live collision shape (11/11 pass); RED-proved via `git stash` on the lib fix alone before restoring. AC-3 full-corpus scan: 0 non-sentinel `nso_ts_key` results with an implausible year prefix across every live `docs/agent-memory/notebooks/*.md` heading. AC-4 data-recovery: `TASK-COWORK-MUTEX-001`'s destroyed CHANGES_REQUESTED verdict survived intact in that row's own `.note` field (only the notebook copy was lost) — reconstructed onto its empty `.status_note` via `orch-apply.sh`. No `apps/` TS/Go touched, no handoff file (BOUNDED-1 direct-execute mint, no PM decomposition) — deviation documented per `main.md`'s Known-drift note.

## [Developer] 2026-08-14 — FIX-BCTC-REPARSE-PERIOD-KEY-SYSTEMATICALLY-STALE-100PCT-QUARANTINE (apps/mcp-server/, dev-mcp-server zone, P0 M, Ready-Lane Consumer dispatch): fixed `reparseSingleWithOcrFallback` (`bctcReparseJob.ts`) — after a 40h cron gap it quarantined 19/19 attempts on period-mismatch, in every case the supplied period strictly EARLIER than the document's own content (e.g. FRT 2024-Q1 supplied vs 2024-Q4 content). Root cause: the function derived its "supplied" period ENTIRELY from `parseYearQuarterFromFilename(payload.filename)` — the stranded queue row's stale filename — and never consulted the extracted `rawText` before calling the pipeline; `parseBctcReport`'s `checkPeriodContentConsistency` guard (correct, untouched) then refused to store under either key, 0% completion, backlog could never drain. ## [Developer] 2026-08-23 — FIX-AUDITOR-C04-PARSEDAT-RECENCY-PREDICATE (cross-service/, developer, P2 S): fixed three system-auditor DB-integrity predicates in the same "timestamp/window doesn't mean what the check assumes" defect family. **C-04** (implementing the architect plan `docs/handoffs/FIX-AUDITOR-C04-PARSEDAT-RECENCY-PREDICATE-spec.md`): moved off `main.md`'s inline SQL entirely into a new `scripts/auditor-db-checks.sh` — recency now anchors on `COALESCE(published_at, parsed_at)` (`published_at` is immutable, frozen at first insert, excluded from the reparse `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`; `parsed_at` is re-stamped on every reparse) instead of the old `parsed_at`-only mutation-timestamp bug; population excludes `validation_status IN ('pending','pending_extraction')` never-extracted shell rows without excluding genuine zero-confidence extraction failures; threshold is now RATE-with-volume-floor (`extracted_total_window>=20 AND lowconf_rate_pct>15` → WARN) replacing the old absolute `≤5` count. New `scripts/auditor-db-checks.test.sh`, 29/29 pass (incl. a replay of the real 2026-07-19/20 89-row/6.74% historical false-positive batch, now correctly PASS). Live verified: `extracted_total_window=1, lowconf_count_window=0, verdict=PASS`; all-time baseline 10/221=4.5%. Found+fixed a real bug while building the script: `awk printf "%.2f"` under this host's `LC_NUMERIC=fr_FR.UTF-8` emits a comma decimal separator, corrupting the JSON — forced `LC_NUMERIC=C` on both awk calls. **C-01/C-02/C-14** (window-only fix, not part of the C-04 spec but same file/same defect family, PO-verified live 2026-07-31 947-vs-129 report): `daily_ohlcv WHERE date >= date('now',<WINDOW>)` → `date = date('now',<WINDOW>)` — the open-ended `>=` bound spanned every date from the anchor day through today (picking up the nightly-backfill-converged prior day's full ~900-1000-code universe on top of the live intraday session), never measuring the single intended trading day at any threshold. Live-verified today (2026-08-23): isolating the operator alone on a weekday-shape replay (anchor 2026-08-19) showed `>=` spanning 2 days = 1015 codes/1796 rows vs `=` single day = 904/904 — same over-count mechanism as the July field report, smaller scale (no nightly-backfill-vs-live-tick day-boundary gap active today). Also fixed an adjacent bug discovered live while verifying on a Sunday: the weekend guard's single `-3 day` literal for "Sat or Sun" never resolves to Friday for either day (Sat needs -1, Sun needs -2) — split into two branches. **Invariant documented** (do NOT build off this): `daily_ohlcv` same-day coverage ~130 codes is BY DESIGN (live intraday tick universe), the full ~940-code universe lands only via the nightly backfill and clobbers `updated_at` — a same-day-vs-trailing-median alarm on `updated_at` would fire every day; explicitly written into `main.md` to stop a future auditor re-chasing this as a "coverage collapse". `docs/policies/dev-standards.md` new CANONICAL entry for `scripts/auditor-db-checks.sh`. No `apps/` TS/Go touched — shell + docs only, `bun test`/`tsc` N/A.

## [Developer] 2026-08-23 — FIX-COWORK-GUARANTEED-SLOT-FIRER-NO-FAILURE-ESCALATION (cross-service/, developer, P0 S): `scripts/agents-flow/cowork-guaranteed-slot-firer.sh` had zero failure-escalation path — `run_firer()` correctly returned non-zero and nothing consumed it. Measured 2026-08-11: 8 guaranteed slots × 3 days, 14 consecutive `exit_code=1` claude-CLI invocations, ZERO BUG alerts, while `launchctl` reported the job healthy throughout (healthy JOB / 100%-failing WORK); the outage was found by a human noticing a missing Facebook post two days late. Added `_escalate_failure()` — a direct `curl` POST to the Telegram BUG channel (mandatory, not a shortcut: this script has no gateway/MCP access and the flow-level `send_telegram` never runs because the CLI dies before Step 0 of any flow). Fires on ALL THREE non-zero outcomes (matcher command failure, unparseable matcher output, ≥1 failing slot), exactly once per tick, never once per slot. Cooldown is time AND content based (`ALERT_STATE_FILE` 2-line epoch+fingerprint, `ALERT_COOLDOWN_SECONDS` default 6h): an unchanged failure re-alerts at most once per window, a NEW failure signature alerts immediately — a pure TTL would have blinded the channel to a new episode. `--dry-run` never escalates; missing creds → `ESCALATION-BLOCKED`, failed POST → `ESCALATION-SEND-FAILED`, never swallowed. **Naming-drift correction:** the row/handoff/architect note all specified `TELEGRAM_BUG_CHAT_ID` — that key does not exist in `.env` (live key is `TELEGRAM_REPORT_BUG_CHANNEL_ID`), so implementing the AC literally would have shipped a silently-dead escalation; `_bug_chat_id()` accepts both, real key first. Folded items closed in the same pass, per the row's own instruction: (a) the invocation log printed `-p 'slot=<id>'` while executing `-p "$trigger_prompt"`, actively misleading triage into a wrong root cause; (b) every log line was written twice because `log()` teed to `$LOG_FILE` while launchd's `StandardOutPath` for this job IS that same file — `log()` now appends directly and echoes to stdout only on an interactive TTY. Test: `bash scripts/agents-flow/cowork-guaranteed-slot-firer.test.sh` 53/53 GREEN (RED baseline 38/15 before the fix), hermetic — fake `CLAUDE_BIN` + fake `CURL_BIN`, zero real CLI invocations, zero network. Live-verified: real `--dry-run` matched the genuinely-due `digest-sunday` slot with zero stdout; a controlled failure against the real `.env` produced one masked POST to the real BUG chat id and the repeat tick was cooldown-suppressed. Docs: `docs/standards/cron-jobs.md`, `docs/policies/dev-standards.md`, `docs/handoffs/TASK-FIX-COWORK-FIRER-ESCALATION.md`. FLAGGED, not fixed (out of file scope): `docs/data/system-map.json` `.telegram_channels[].env_var` names three keys that do not exist in `.env` — needs its own row.
